| `developer_appreciation` | 27 | 4.46% | — | very strong |
| `cant_find_delete_edit` / `bug_watch_loading` | 23 each | 3.80% | — | very strong |
| `bug_keyboard_memo` | 22 | 3.64% | 0.00% | very strong |
| `ads_complaint` | 15 | **2.48%** | **9.20%** | meaningful |
| `ads_gate_habit_creation` | 17 | 2.81% | 5.17% | meaningful |
| `free_tier_cap` | 10 | **1.65%** | **5.75%** | meaningful |
| `price_too_high` | 6 | **0.99%** | **8.05%** | emerging |
| `churn_uninstalled` | 13 | **2.15%** | **6.90%** | meaningful |

**Four Korea-specific findings, all of them consequential:**

**(a) Koreans use the review box as a support channel.** `question_to_developer` is **8.10% of Korean reviews and 0.57% of everyone else's** — a 14× difference, and the single largest national behavioural divergence in the corpus. Forty-nine Korean reviewers ask the developer something directly, usually politely, usually at 5★, and usually about something the app already does. Combined with `cant_find_delete_edit` (23 KR) and `onboarding_no_guidance` (18 KR), **65 distinct Korean reviewers (10.74% of the Korean corpus) are asking for help rather than reviewing.**

**(b) Koreans notice releases.** `update_regression` is 8.76% of Korean reviews against 3.45% elsewhere. This is partly real (the Korean base is older and more tenured — 17 of the 23 `long_tenure_user` reviews are Korean) and partly attentional: Korean reviewers write when something changes, in both directions (`feature_shipped_praise` is 5 of 7 Korean).

**(c) Koreans complain far less about money and far more about breakage.** Price is 0.99% of Korean reviews against 8.05% of non-Korean; ads 2.48% against 9.20%; the free-tier cap 1.65% against 5.75%. Meanwhile `update_regression`, `bug_widget_not_showing` and `bug_keyboard_memo` are all Korea-heavy. **Interpretation (labelled as interpretation, and deliberately narrow): this corpus shows a difference in what each group writes about, not a proven difference in what each group feels. A plausible in-corpus explanation is tenure — Korean reviewers have been on the product longer, have often already paid (12.89%), and therefore write about regressions rather than about pricing they already accepted.** The corpus cannot distinguish this from a cultural difference and does not try.

**(d) Cross-device is a Korean demand.** 37 of the 44 sync requests, **all 13** Android requests, **all 8** Mac requests, and 18 of 21 iPad requests are Korean. *"제발 갤럭시도 쓸 수 있게 플레이 스토어에도 이 어플 내주세요ㅜㅜ"* / "please release this on Play Store so Galaxy users can use it too" (`9091557818`); *"역시 앱은 국산이다, 그러나.."* ⁄ *"맥용은 커녕 아이패드 용이 없다는게 치명적 단점.."* / "Korean apps really are the best — but having no Mac version, let alone iPad, is a fatal flaw" (`9800909083`).

## 6.3 United States — n = 66 (8.47% of corpus), mean 3.727 · **eligible**

| Theme | n | % of 66 | vs KR | Signal (US denominator) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `simplicity_minimal` | 13 | 19.70% | 10.74% | high-priority |
| `comparative_best` | 12 | 18.18% | 7.93% | high-priority |
| `paid_premium_confirmed` | 11 | 16.67% | 12.89% | high-priority |
| **`ads_complaint`** | **10** | **15.15%** | **2.48%** | high-priority |
| **`churn_uninstalled`** | **10** | **15.15%** | **2.15%** | high-priority |
| `feature_completeness_praise` | 10 | 15.15% | 3.31% | high-priority |
| `outcome_behavior_change` | 9 | 13.64% | 4.96% | high-priority |
| `aesthetic_design` | 7 | 10.61% | 7.77% | high-priority |
| `ease_of_use` / `stats_reports_praise` | 6 each | 9.09% | — | high-priority |
| `ads_gate_habit_creation` | 5 | 7.58% | 2.81% | high-priority |
| `advocacy_recommend` | 5 | 7.58% | 2.64% | high-priority |
| `lifetime_purchase_praise` | 4 | 6.06% | 0.33% | high-priority |
| `free_tier_cap` / `free_praise` / `long_tenure_user` | 4 each | 6.06% | — | high-priority |
| `monetization_model_change_complaint` | 3 | 4.55% | 0.33% | very strong |

**The US corpus is bimodal and the split is clean.** Half of it is the strongest advocacy in the dataset — 18.18% call it the best they have tried, 13.64% report a behaviour change, 15.15% say it has everything they need, and **4 of the corpus's 8 lifetime-model praises are American**. The other half is the ad reaction: **`ads_complaint` at 15.15% and `churn_uninstalled` at 15.15%, both roughly 6× the Korean rate**, with a mean of 3.727 against Korea's 4.225.

*"This app has every feature I was looking for"* (`9152772777`) sits in the same corpus as *"Ad vomit … I deleted this trash app immediately"* (`9783504520`). **Interpretation: the United States is the market where the free-tier experience decides the outcome, and the ad gate is deciding it badly. Ten American reviewers state they uninstalled; eight of them blame ads.**

**Both `adhd_neurodivergent` reviews in the corpus are American, and they disagree** — `6914248951` (2★, 2021: *"I downloaded this to help myself manage my adhd struggles but as soon as I made a single activity scheduled it gave me an add"*) and `11929068665` (5★, 2024: *"The color coding and the variety of views has been amazingly functional for my ADHD"*). **At n = 2 this is a weak signal (0.26%) and no segment claim is made from it** — but the pairing is instructive: same audience, opposite verdict, and the difference is the ad.

## 6.4 Japan — n = 26 ⚠️ **limited evidence (24 below the threshold)**, mean 3.692

**Reported because it is the most monetization-dense storefront in the corpus, and flagged as limited evidence every time it is used.**

`price_too_high` is **7 of 26 Japanese reviews (26.92% of JP)** against 2.57% globally. Two of the seven (`10860902855`, `11879711185`) explicitly document the ¥1,600 → ¥4,500 rise; one proposes a ¥2,000 tier (`11510979900`); one would have paid ¥300 (`8685490868`). `free_tier_cap` is 3/26 (11.54%), `paywall_feature_gating` 2/26, `price_increase_complaint` 2/26 — **and both price-increase reviews in the entire corpus are Japanese.**

Japan is also the only storefront where **`onboarding_no_guidance` (4/26, 15.38%) and `localization_gap` (2/26)** cluster: *"アイデアをタップして出てくる追加項目は日本語になっていない。設定の中にもちょこちょこ英語とハングルまざってるから"* / "the suggestions that appear when you tap Ideas aren't in Japanese, and settings has English and Korean mixed in here and there" (`11187701142`, 3★); *"英語で説明を書かれても分かりません💦 直感的に機能がわかるなら問題ないのですが、シンプルな画面故に分からない"* / "I can't understand explanations written in English. It'd be fine if the features were self-evident, but the screen is so minimal that they aren't" (`11379147577`, 1★). **Interpretation, clearly labelled as limited evidence: Japan is simultaneously the most price-sensitive and the least-localised storefront in the corpus. Those two facts are unlikely to be independent.**

Japan also supplies the corpus's only **positive iCloud-sync report** (`11710806302`) and its only storage-bloat report (`11805553961`: *"this app uses over 10GB of my storage"*).

## 6.5 Other sub-50 storefronts — limited evidence only

No standalone claim is made for any storefront below. Listed where a specific, checkable observation exists.

| Storefront | n | Observation | IDs |
|---|---|---|---|
| Canada | 12 | **3 of the corpus's 27 `cant_find_delete_edit` reviews** — an unusually high share for 12 reviews; also the most detailed unbundling proposal | `8787931317`, `9145622375`, `9562235582`, `7028234235` |
| Great Britain | 10 | Mean 4.300, the highest of any storefront with n ≥ 10; one Korean-language review filed on the GB storefront | `11143057029` |
| Mexico | 7 | Contains both a data-loss report and the Family-Sharing mis-sell | `10740823617`, `10724795076` |
| Germany | 6 | Mean 4.500; the only reviewer who praises the timeline/print feature as unique in the category | `12574741140` |
| China | 5 | **3 of 5 ask for a lower buyout price**; 1 reports incomplete Simplified Chinese | `5627754008`, `6075694865`, `10849290037`, `11309205520` |
| Russia | 3 | Supplies the corpus's longest and most structured feature proposal (1,941 characters) — skipped-vs-failed day distinction and streak grouping | `13574542005` |
| Singapore / Pakistan / Hong Kong | 2 / 1 / 3 | **Three of the ten `monetization_model_change_complaint` reviews come from these three tiny storefronts** — the pattern is geographically wide even where counts are tiny | `13386826119`, `12795320709`, `14352377997` |
| Poland | 3 | The only reviewer in the corpus who asks for a *subscription* instead of a lifetime price | `10639075443` |
| Austria / Czechia | 1 / 2 | Both 1★ reviews are ad-gate uninstalls, the earliest non-Korean examples | `10767699701`, `8077608265` |
| India | 3 | Mean 5.000; all three praise the free tier's generosity **in 2020–2021, before the cap tightened** | `6115842538`, `7063081300`, `7925046804` |

## 6.6 High-spend market group

**Definition and disclosure:** membership is taken from publicly reported App Store consumer-spend market rankings, accessed 12 September 2026 (external, **low–medium confidence**, §9.5). Group = **US, JP, GB, DE, FR, CA, AU, KR, CN**. France contributes 0 reviews.

| Group | n | % of 779 | Mean ★ | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| High-spend group (incl. KR) | 733 | 94.10% | 4.158 | Indistinguishable from the corpus — because Korea is 83% of the group |
| **High-spend group excluding KR** | **128** | 16.43% | **3.844** | `simplicity_minimal` 18.8% · `paid_premium_confirmed` 13.3% · `ads_complaint` 9.4% · `price_too_high` 8.6% · `churn_uninstalled` 7.8% |
| Rest of world (not KR/US/JP) | 82 | 10.53% | 3.927 | `simplicity_minimal` 18.3% · `ease_of_use` 15.9% · `aesthetic_design` 14.6% · `intent_to_purchase` 7.3% |

**The high-spend group is analytically useless unless Korea is removed**, because Korea is 82.5% of it. With Korea removed, the group's profile is: **more praise for simplicity, the same payer share, and roughly triple the ads and price friction.** Mean rating 3.844 against Korea's 4.225.

**Interpretation: the markets with the most money to spend are the markets where the free-tier experience is doing the most damage.** That is the strongest commercial argument in this report for moving the interstitial.

## 6.7 High-review-volume market group

**Defined from this corpus only, as a disclosed proxy. This is review volume, not downloads, and must not be described as downloads.** Group = storefronts with ≥ 25 reviews: **Korea (605), United States (66), Japan (26) = 697 reviews, 89.47% of the corpus.**

| | KR (605) | US (66) | JP (26) ⚠️ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mean ★ | 4.225 | 3.727 | 3.692 |
| Payer share | 12.89% | 16.67% | 15.38% |
| Ads complaint | 2.48% | 15.15% | 0.00% |
| Price too high | 0.99% | 1.52% | **26.92%** |
| Free-tier cap | 1.65% | 6.06% | 11.54% |
| Stated uninstall | 2.15% | 15.15% | 0.00% |
| Question to developer | 8.10% | 0.00% | 3.85% |
| Comparative best | 7.93% | 18.18% | 0.00% |
| Cross-device request (incl. Android/Mac) | 12.23% | 7.58% | 0.00% |

**Three distinct market problems, one product:**
- **Korea** — the tenured base. Loves it, pays for it, is worn down by regressions and by having nowhere to ask questions. **Risk: quiet attrition, visible as a falling mean rather than as angry reviews.**
- **United States** — the widest gap between advocacy and rejection. **Risk: losing the whole top-of-funnel to a 30-second ad before anyone sees the product.**
- **Japan** ⚠️ (limited evidence) — the most willing to articulate a price they *would* pay, and the most alienated by the price they are asked for. **Risk: a market that would convert at a lower tier converts at zero instead.**

## 6.8 Global comparison — what is uniform and what is not

**Uniform across every storefront with ≥ 5 reviews:** praise for simplicity, ease of use and visual design; widget trouble; the wish for cross-device sync. These three themes appear in Korean, English, Japanese, Chinese, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Hungarian reviews alike.

**Not uniform — and the divergences are large:**

| Theme | KR (605) | Non-KR (174) | Ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| `question_to_developer` | 8.10% | 0.57% | **14.2×** |
| `price_too_high` | 0.99% | 8.05% | **0.12×** |
| `ads_complaint` | 2.48% | 9.20% | **0.27×** |
| `free_tier_cap` | 1.65% | 5.75% | **0.29×** |
| `churn_uninstalled` | 2.15% | 6.90% | **0.31×** |
| `update_regression` | 8.76% | 3.45% | **2.5×** |
| `simplicity_minimal` | 10.74% | 17.24% | 0.62× |
| `comparative_best` | 7.93% | 12.07% | 0.66× |

**The single sentence version: outside Korea, people complain about what the app costs them; inside Korea, people complain about what the app broke.** Both are real, both are actionable, and a roadmap built only on the global numbers would optimise for the Korean failure mode and leave the international one untouched.

---

# PART 7 — TIME-TREND ANALYSIS

## 7.1 Method

Two cuts are used and both are disclosed.

- **By calendar year** (2019 partial from 30 March; 2026 partial to 27 August). Denominators: 68 / 179 / 139 / 92 / 88 / 104 / 72 / 37.
- **Three eras**, chosen so each has ≥ 180 records: **E1 = Mar 2019 – Dec 2021 (n = 386)**, **E2 = Jan 2022 – Dec 2023 (n = 180)**, **E3 = Jan 2024 – Aug 2026 (n = 213)**. Era means: **4.301 / 4.211 / 3.765**.

**Three constraints apply to every trend below.** (1) There is no version field, so nothing can be tied to a release except through reviewer text. (2) The 2020 defect bursts (§1.4) inflate that year's reliability share. (3) 2026 has only 37 reviews; every 2026 percentage moves by 2.7 points per review. Trends are labelled with a confidence level and none is asserted beyond what the counts support.

## 7.2 Trend 1 — The rating decline is real, recent and monotonic · **worsening, high confidence**

| Year | 2019 | 2020 | 2021 | 2022 | 2023 | 2024 | 2025 | 2026 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mean ★ | 4.368 | 4.285 | 4.288 | **4.380** | 4.034 | 3.971 | 3.708 | **3.297** |
| n | 68 | 179 | 139 | 92 | 88 | 104 | 72 | 37 |

**Flat-to-rising for four years, then four consecutive falls totalling 1.08 stars from the 2022 peak.** Era means confirm it: 4.301 → 4.211 → 3.765. The 1★ share rises from 5.9% (2019) to **29.7% (2026)**; the 5★ share falls from 63.2% to **37.8%**.

**The decline is not driven by volume decay.** 2024 had *more* reviews than 2023 (104 vs 88) and a lower mean. **Confidence: high for the direction, medium for the 2026 magnitude (n = 37).**

## 7.3 Trend 2 — Monetization friction more than quadrupled · **worsening, high confidence**

| Family | E1 (386) | E2 (180) | E3 (213) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Monetization friction (any)** | 20 (5.18%) | 19 (10.56%) | **50 (23.47%)** |
| `free_tier_cap` | 3 (0.78%) | 1 (0.56%) | **16 (7.51%)** |
| `price_too_high` | 2 (0.52%) | 6 (3.33%) | **12 (5.63%)** |
| `post_purchase_failure` | 2 (0.52%) | 6 (3.33%) | **14 (6.57%)** |
| `restore_purchase_failure` | 0 | 0 | **8 (3.76%)** |
| `monetization_model_change_complaint` | 1 (0.26%) | 1 (0.56%) | **8 (3.76%)** |
| **Paid-user trouble (union)** | 5 (1.30%) | 9 (5.00%) | **22 (10.33%)** |

**Nearly a quarter of every review written since January 2024 contains a monetization complaint, against one in twenty before 2022.** Every component rose; two (`restore_purchase_failure`, and effectively `monetization_model_change_complaint`) did not exist before 2024 at all.

**This is the clearest single explanation available for Trend 1.** It is not a proof of causation — the corpus cannot establish that — but the timing, the direction and the 1★ concentration all align. **Confidence: high.**

## 7.4 Trend 3 — The ad gate hardened and then started failing · **worsening, high confidence**

| Theme | E1 (386) | E2 (180) | E3 (213) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ads_complaint` | 10 (2.59%) | 11 (6.11%) | 10 (4.69%) |
| `ads_gate_habit_creation` | 5 (1.30%) | 8 (4.44%) | **13 (6.10%)** |
| `ads_broken` | 1 (0.26%) | 0 | **7 (3.29%)** |
| **Ads (any)** | 11 (2.85%) | 14 (7.78%) | **22 (10.33%)** |

**Three distinct phases, each visible in the text:**
1. **2019–2020:** ads exist and are mostly banner. One reviewer in Jan 2021 still praises the app for having *none* (`6934202676`: *"광고도 없고 너무 좋음"* / "no ads and it's great"), which dates the interstitial's arrival.
2. **2021–2023:** the creation gate appears and lengthens. Reviewers quote **5 seconds** in 2021 (`7877990468`, `8110196858`), **30 seconds** from Sept 2023 (`10353308106`, `10614381628`).
3. **2024–2026:** the gate starts failing. **7 of the 8 `ads_broken` reviews are in E3**, and the most recent is 24 August 2026 (`14465530297`) — three days before the corpus ends.

**Confidence: high for the trend; the phase boundaries are dated from reviewer text and are approximate.**

## 7.5 Trend 4 — Widget defects became the dominant reliability problem · **worsening, high confidence**

| Family | E1 (386) | E2 (180) | E3 (213) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Any reliability defect** | 116 (30.05%) | 41 (22.78%) | **72 (33.80%)** |
| **Widget defects only** | 33 (8.55%) | 18 (10.00%) | **39 (18.31%)** |
| `bug_widget_not_showing` | 15 (3.89%) | 12 (6.67%) | **23 (10.80%)** |
| `bug_widget_group_deleted` | 0 | 0 | **9 (4.23%)** |
| `bug_app_crash` | 13 (3.37%) | **0** | 5 (2.35%) |
| `bug_watch_loading` | 19 (4.92%) | 5 (2.78%) | **2 (0.94%)** |
| `bug_keyboard_memo` | 22 (5.70%) | 0 | 0 |
| `bug_data_loss` | 3 (0.78%) | 1 (0.56%) | **8 (3.76%)** |

**The total defect rate is roughly flat across seven years (30% → 23% → 34%) but its composition inverted.** E1's problems were the app itself (crashes, keyboard, Watch) and they were **fixed** — `bug_keyboard_memo` and `bug_reminder_time` vanish after 2020, `bug_app_crash` is zero for the whole of 2022–2023, `bug_watch_loading` falls from 4.92% to 0.94%. E3's problems are the widget (18.31%, more than doubling E1) and data loss (3.76%, up 5×).

**Interpretation: this team demonstrably can fix hard defects — the 2020 keyboard and crash clusters both stop dead. The widget is the one that has not been fixed, and it is now carrying the largest share of the negative signal.** **Confidence: high.**

## 7.6 Trend 5 — Advocacy and displacement collapsed · **worsening, high confidence**

| Theme | E1 (386) | E2 (180) | E3 (213) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comparative_best` | 40 (10.36%) | 20 (11.11%) | **9 (4.23%)** |
| `outcome_behavior_change` | 28 (7.25%) | 10 (5.56%) | **5 (2.35%)** |
| `simplicity_minimal` | 57 (14.77%) | 22 (12.22%) | **16 (7.51%)** |
| **UX praise (any)** | 109 (28.24%) | 48 (26.67%) | **41 (19.25%)** |
| **Named-feature praise (any)** | 69 (17.88%) | 36 (20.00%) | **30 (14.08%)** |

**"This is the best habit app I've tried" more than halved. "This changed my behaviour" fell by two-thirds.** These are the two themes most closely tied to word of mouth, and both decayed while the paid share rose.

**A partial counter-reading, stated honestly:** `ease_of_use` (10.36 → 11.11 → 9.86%) and `aesthetic_design` (9.33 → 7.22 → 9.39%) are essentially flat. **Users have not stopped finding the product easy or attractive. They have stopped calling it the best and stopped reporting that it worked on them.** **Confidence: high for the magnitude of the `comparative_best` and `outcome_behavior_change` declines; medium for the interpretation.**

## 7.7 Trend 6 — Some things were genuinely fixed, and the corpus records it · **improving, high confidence**

This is the report's most encouraging finding and it deserves equal weight with the negative trends.

| What changed | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **Streaks shipped**, Jan–Feb 2020 | Requested `5360484203` (4 Jan 2020) → shipped and thanked `5516233972` (11 Feb 2020: *"streak 기능 생겼네요ㅜㅠ 개발자 님 사랑합니다"* / "the streak feature arrived! I love you, developer") |
| **Dropbox backup shipped**, ~Jan 2020 | `5353897655`: *"<6개월 사용> 백업기능이 생겨서 너무 좋습니다"* / "(6 months in) so glad backup arrived" |
| **Apple Watch app shipped**, ~Mar 2020 | Requested `4858004385`, `5216546907`, `5591618893` → shipped and thanked `5621508960` (*"워치에 있는거 보고 감동"* / "moved to see it on the Watch") |
| **The custom widget was restored after users complained** | `7224428696` edits their own review: *"(수정) 사용자화 다시 생겨서 너무 좋아욤!!! 감사합니당"* / "(edit) the custom widget is back, I love it, thank you" |
| **`widget_tap_opens_app` disappears after Dec 2023** | 8 reviews in E1, 8 in E2, **0 in E3** |
| **Past-date check-in went from top complaint to differentiator** | `past_date_checkin_difficulty` 11 of 13 in 2019–2020 → `past_date_checkin_praise` 4 reviews, mean ★ 5.000, 2020–2022 (§3.3.5) |
| **`bug_keyboard_memo` and `bug_reminder_time` stop dead after Sept 2020** | 22 and 4 reviews, all within weeks; zero afterwards |
| **`accidental_purchase` stops after Jan 2023** | 6 reviews, all 2019–2023 — consistent with a purchase-sheet fix |
| **Skip/rest-day shipped**, mid-2024 | `11410069153` (22 Jun 2024): *"I'm really pleased that I can use this feature, which I've been eagerly waiting for"* |
| **iCloud sync worked for at least one user**, Sept 2024 | `11710806302` (JP) |
| **A widget fix landed and a payer noticed**, Nov 2024 | `11929068665` (US, 5★): *"Recent update is a godsend. They fixed an issue with the widgets 🫶 and this app is back to being one of my top 3 apps"* |

**Interpretation: seven of the ten largest complaints in this corpus's first three years were addressed. The problem is not that this team does not fix things — it is that what they have shipped since 2024 has been monetization tightening, while the widget, sync and data-integrity problems have not moved.**

## 7.8 Trend 7 — The demand for cross-device never moved · **persistent, high confidence**

| | E1 (386) | E2 (180) | E3 (213) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cross-device / platform gap (union) | 29 (7.51%) | 28 (15.56%) | 27 (12.68%) |
| `req_icloud_sync` | 13 (3.37%) | 18 (10.00%) | 13 (6.10%) |

**Twenty-nine, twenty-eight, twenty-seven.** Seven years, three eras, essentially the same absolute number of people asking for the same thing. The first request is `4135463077` (11 May 2019: *"아이패드나 아이폰 연동을 위한 백업 복구가 되었으면 좋겠어요!"*); the last is `13912612870` (2 Apr 2026, a Premium user: *"프리미엄으로 구매해서 사용중인데, 아이패드는 연동이 안되는거같아서 문의남깁니다"* / "I bought Premium and iPad doesn't seem to sync — asking here").

**This is the longest-running unresolved demand in the corpus and the only one where a reviewer explicitly accuses the team of stalling**: *"아이패드와 맥북 호환여부를 오래전부터 질문 한 분들이 많은데 매번 예정이라고 하던데 진짜 되긴 하나요?"* (`11361971544`). **Confidence: high.**

## 7.9 Trend 8 — Payers became a much larger share of reviewers · **emerging, medium confidence**

`paid_premium_confirmed` by era: **31 of 386 (8.03%) → 22 of 180 (12.22%) → 46 of 213 (21.60%)**. In 2026 alone it is **12 of 37 (32.43%)**.

**Two readings, and the corpus supports both:**
- **Benign:** the paywall is doing its job; more reviewers are customers.
- **Adverse:** as §5.4 shows, the *reason* payers write has shifted. 12.9% of E1 payers reported a purchase failure; **45.7% of E3 payers do.** The rising payer share in reviews may be measuring anger, not adoption.

**The 2026 slice settles it in favour of the adverse reading for that year specifically: of the 12 self-identified payers in 2026, 8 report a post-purchase failure.** **Confidence: medium — the underlying share of payers among *users* is unknowable from this corpus.**

## 7.10 What did not change

- **The praise vocabulary.** *깔끔* / *심플* / *직관적* / "simple" / "clean" appear at comparable rates in 2019 and 2026.
- **The request list.** Sync, iPad, multiple daily check-ins, notes on missed days, past reports — all present in 2019 and all present in 2026.
- **`ease_of_use` and `aesthetic_design`**, flat across all three eras.
- **`question_to_developer`** (6.99% → 5.00% → 6.57%) — Koreans have been using the review box as a help desk for the entire life of the product.
- **`update_regression`** (7.51% → 6.11% → 8.92%) — the "an update broke it" complaint has been a constant of this product for seven years.

---

# PART 8 — PRODUCT IMPLICATIONS

Every recommendation below names the findings it rests on. Immediate fixes are separated from structural changes, experiments and open questions.

## 8.1 Immediate fixes — the evidence is unambiguous

**1. Close out the Premium-activation failure and say so publicly.** *(§5.5, §5.4, §2.3)*
Eight reviews in five days, six of them 1★, one still failing on 3.1.1, externally corroborated as a 3.1.0 defect. **Verify that 3.1.1 actually resolves it for the users who reported otherwise (`14358584372`), and post a response on the affected reviews.** Six of the eight are 1★ from paying customers; three of them (`14340812621`, `14346556200`, `13014831178`) explicitly ask for fast action and one offers to raise their rating if it is fixed. **This is the cheapest rating recovery available in the entire corpus.**

**2. Fix the restore-purchase path.** *(§5.4)*
8 reviewers lose their purchase on reinstall or device change (`10774120558`, `11237191728`, `11551843035`, `11753525633`, `11814795489`, `14340621141`, `14351975658`, `14358584372`); 3 more never had entitlement apply at all (`5370880957`, `6887164010`, `8402874369`). **All 8 restore failures are from 2024 onward.** A payer who loses their purchase and sees ads again (`11551843035`) is the worst experience this product can deliver.

**3. Fix "widget does not appear" and the phantom "group deleted".** *(§3.3.2)*
50 and 9 reviews respectively; the first is rising every era (3.89% → 6.67% → 10.80%); 23 of 99 payers are affected. The "group deleted" defect is well-bounded (Apr 2024 – Dec 2025) and has a reproduction in `12069773156`. **58 of 90 widget-defect reviews are 4★ or 5★ — these users are still here and can be recovered.**

**4. Make "Display on" actually filter, or remove it.** *(§3.4)*
16 reviewers requested per-weekday display; 8 report the shipped feature ignoring their selection, from 2020 to 2025 (`6417185599`, `8566400973`, `9511684630`, `9584258433`, `9672617646`, `11318555804`, `11929954675`, `12807350928`). **A feature that silently fails keeps generating the request that produced it.**

**5. Make delete and edit discoverable.** *(§3.3.5)*
27 reviewers could not find it; the function exists and a user had to document it publicly (`7781598096`). A swipe action or a visible button on the habit detail view removes a theme that produced 5 one-star reviews and 10 four-star reviews. `9562235582` (CA) names the exact fix: *"deleting a habit is almost hidden — it should be a clear option when viewing the habit detailed view"*

**6. Put a support link and an FAQ where Korean users are already asking.** *(§3.3.6, §6.2)*
49 Korean reviewers ask the developer a question inside a review; 6 say they could not find how to make contact; 8 say they got no reply. 65 distinct Korean reviewers — 10.74% of the Korean corpus — are asking for help rather than reviewing. **Most of these are 5★ reviews asking how to do something the app already does.**

**7. Ship an in-app changelog before the next tier change.** *(§3.3.7, §4.4, §7.3)*
`monetization_model_change_complaint` has the lowest mean of any theme with n ≥ 5 (**1.700**), and the common thread is surprise, not price: *"おしらせにもないし、どうなっているのでしょうか…？"* / "there wasn't even an announcement — what's going on?" (`13169190954`). Two-star reviews are 8× over-indexed on this theme, and they come from the app's oldest users.

## 8.2 Structural decisions — direction is clear, magnitude is not

**8. Move the interstitial off habit creation.** *(§3.3.1, §4.5, §6.3, §6.6)*
This is the highest-value change in the report and also the one with the most revenue risk, so the evidence is stated precisely: 47 reviewers (6.03%) complain about ads, at mean ★ 2.234; 26 name the creation gate specifically; **20 of the 25 stated uninstalls (80%) cite ads**; the ads rate is 15.15% in the US against 2.48% in Korea; and the **six reviewers who tolerate ads all describe them as short and confined to creation** — every one of them gave 5★.

**The corpus contains the design answer as well as the problem.** Reviewers distinguish between banner ads (accepted) and a 30-second interstitial at setup (rejected), and between an ad on creation (tolerable at 5s) and an ad on every memo (`11543927654`: *"한번매모할때 마다 29초 광고"*). **Options the evidence supports, in order:** shorten the interstitial back to ~5 seconds; move it to a session boundary rather than the create action; or gate it after the *n*th habit rather than the *3rd–4th* (multiple reviewers name 3 or 4 as the trigger point: `10476012561`, `12474152923`, `13878772549`).

**9. Decide what "lifetime" meant, and honour it.** *(§2.2, §5.5, §5.2)*
Eight reviewers bought explicitly because it was a one-time purchase, four of them American, and `6636232220` (ES) wrote three sentences on why subscriptions felt abusive *before* buying. A subscription now exists and at least one lifetime buyer reports being asked to subscribe (`14352377997`, HK, 1★, *"DO NOT BUY IT"*). **Whatever the commercial decision, the migration needs an explicit, published grandfathering policy. One public "DO NOT BUY IT" on the storefront costs more than the subscription it was protesting.**

**10. Resolve cross-device — ship it, or say clearly that it will not ship.** *(§3.4, §7.8, §6.2)*
72–84 reviewers across seven years; flat demand; one reviewer accusing the team of repeatedly saying "planned". The corpus shows one successful iCloud report (JP, Sept 2024) against six later Korean reviewers saying it does not work. **Either it is Premium-gated and undiscoverable — in which case say so in the paywall copy — or it is partial, in which case the roadmap statement is overdue.** An iPad build is a separate ask (21 reviewers, 18 Korean) and cheaper.

**11. Unbundle Premium, or introduce a trial, for the price-resistant markets.** *(§5.6, §6.4, §6.7)*
Price objection is 8.05% outside Korea and 0.99% inside it; Japan is 26.92% (limited evidence, n = 26). Three reviewers on three continents independently proposed the same fix — a cheaper tier covering ad removal + groups + backup (`7028234235` CA, `11510979900` JP, `10639075443` PL) — and three more asked for a trial. **The corpus cannot tell you the revenue effect. It can tell you that the specific ask is consistent across seven years and three markets, and that ¥2,000 and "under ₩20,000" are the numbers reviewers name.**

**12. Treat backup as a safety feature, not a paid one.** *(§3.3.4)*
12 data-loss reports, 8 since 2024, 6 at 1★ — and `11222810326` and `11103760055` both discovered only at the moment of loss that backup required payment. **Gating recovery behind a purchase converts a bug into a grievance.** At minimum, an automatic local export, or a one-time free restore after a detected data loss.

## 8.3 Experiments worth running

| Experiment | Hypothesis it tests | Findings behind it | Primary metric |
|---|---|---|---|
| Interstitial length: 30s vs 5s vs none, on the create action | Tolerance is a function of duration, not existence | §3.3.1 — all six `ads_tolerable` reviewers describe a short ad | D1 retention + habit-creation completion rate |
| Move the gate from habit *n=3* to *n=8* | The gate fires before the product has demonstrated value | `10476012561`, `12474152923`, `13878772549`, `12446921299` | Share of installs that create ≥ 3 habits |
| A ~40%-priced tier (ads + groups + backup only) in JP/US/EU | Bundling, not price, is the barrier | §5.6(c), three independent proposals | Revenue per install, not conversion rate |
| 7-day full-feature trial | The free tier is too constrained to evaluate the paid one | `8301510290`, `10850332771`, `12572214309` | Trial→paid, and the `price_too_high` review rate |
| In-app FAQ answering the five most-asked review questions | A measurable share of reviews are support tickets | §6.2(a) — 49 KR questions, mostly about existing features | Volume of `question_to_developer` reviews |
| Proactive changelog + grandfathering notice before any tier change | Surprise, not price, drives the 1.700-mean theme | §7.3, §4.4 | 1–2★ rate in the 30 days after a tier change |

## 8.4 Research questions this corpus raises but cannot answer

1. **What is the actual conversion rate?** 12.71% of *reviewers* say they paid. The corpus cannot convert that into a user-base figure and no reader should try.
2. **Did the interstitial increase or decrease revenue?** The corpus shows 20 uninstalls attributed to it and 8 purchases attributed to removing it. That is not a net figure.
3. **Why is Korea 77.66%?** Localisation, ASO, press, community, or an Android gap pushing Korean iOS users here — the corpus gives no channel attribution at all (§3.6).
4. **Is iCloud sync shipped, Premium-gated, or partial?** One reviewer says it works; six later ones say it does not. A single sentence from the team resolves this.
5. **What happened to the review volume?** 179 reviews in 2020, 37 in 2026. Falling installs, falling prompts, or falling engagement are indistinguishable here.
6. **Do the 1★ ad reviewers ever come back?** No reviewer in the corpus describes reinstalling after an ad-driven uninstall.
7. **What is the current price and model?** Store metadata was unreachable (§2.3). Every price in this report is a reviewer's recollection on a dated review.

## 8.5 What not to change

- **Do not add features to the main surface.** 95 reviewers praise minimalism by name and several plead against expansion: *"복잡하게 뭐 더 안넣고 지금의 간결함을 최대한 유지했으면 좋겠습니다"* (`6416619753`); *"다른 루틴앱은 넘모 복잡함"* / "other routine apps are way too complicated" (`9198233301`); *"Other apps tried to do way too much"* (`8241797483`). The 267 requests in §3.4 should be read as *settings*, not as surface.
- **Do not remove the stamp metaphor or the check-in sound.** 19 + 5 reviewers name them specifically, at means of 4.947 and 3.800.
- **Do not add streak punishment.** Three reviewers explicitly chose this app because it does *not* punish a missed day (§3.5.3), and the most detailed feature proposal in the corpus (`13574542005`, RU) asks for the skipped/failed distinction precisely to preserve that.
- **Do not remove the lifetime option without a grandfathering statement** (§8.2, item 9).
- **Do not re-tighten the free tier before fixing §8.1.** The last three tightenings produced the corpus's lowest-mean theme (1.700) and its 2★ bucket.

---

# PART 9 — EVIDENCE APPENDIX

## 9.1 Theme definitions and boundary rules

**163 themes.** Every theme is a hand-assigned list of `review_id` values. The complete ID lists are at §9.3; the complete per-review index is at §9.6.

**Counting rules:**
- Themes are **non-exclusive**. A review may carry any number; mean 2.44, min 1, max 10.
- The **global denominator is always 779**. Segment rates (e.g. "% of the 99 payers", "% of the 605 Korean reviews", "% of the 72 one-star reviews") are always labelled as segment rates and are never substituted for the global classification.
- A theme is assigned only when the review **states** it. Inference from rating alone is never used.
- **At n = 779, one review = 0.13%**, which lands in the "weak" band. The "ignore" band (<0.1%) is unreachable. **29 themes have n = 1**; each is labelled weak and enumerated by ID, and none is promoted to a finding.

**The boundary rules that mattered:**

| Boundary | Rule applied | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `paid_premium_confirmed` vs `accidental_purchase` | `paid_premium_confirmed` requires a *deliberate* purchase the reviewer stands behind. A mis-tap followed by a refund request is `accidental_purchase` only. | `9541183862` (*"바로 영구결제했어요"*) = payer; `6288381604` (*"실수로 결제"*, wants refund) = `accidental_purchase`, **not** a payer |
| `paid_premium_confirmed` vs `intent_to_purchase` | Stating a future intention is never a purchase. | `10509051172` (*"프리미엄도 고민중"* / "considering Premium") = intent; `10791311772` (*"프리미엄 쓸만한 가치가 있어요"* — speaks as a user of it) = payer |
| `ads_complaint` vs `ads_gate_habit_creation` | The gate theme requires the reviewer to tie the ad to the *create* action. Volume complaints alone are `ads_complaint`. Many carry both. | `10269881878` (*"millions of ads"*) = complaint only; `8077608265` (*"A full screen ad after just creating one habit"*) = both |
| `ads_broken` vs `ads_gate_habit_creation` | `ads_broken` requires the ad itself to fail (not load, not dismiss), blocking the action. | `12589912745` (*"Can't add a task - no ads to show"*) = both; `14240219624` (*"광고 30초"*) = gate only |
| `bug_widget_not_showing` vs `bug_widget_not_updating` | "Not showing" = the widget is absent, blank or black. "Not updating" = the widget renders but its content is stale or incomplete. **This is the most contestable boundary in the map**, which is why §3.3.2 reports the union first. | `10800414807` (*"위젯에 아무것도 안뜨는데 (검정색)"*) = not showing; `7283843934` (*"기존 항목들은 잘 보이는데 하나 더 추가했더니 위젯이 뜨지 않아요"*) = not updating |
| `update_regression` | Requires the reviewer to attribute the change to an update. "It stopped working" alone is not enough. | `6077415989` (*"업뎃이후 어플이 켜지지가 않아요"*) = assigned; `6079977786` (*"깔고 들어가자마자 튕겨서"*) = `bug_app_crash` only |
| `simplicity_minimal` vs `ease_of_use` | `simplicity_minimal` = the product is lean / uncluttered / not overwhelming (*깔끔*, *심플*, *군더더기 없는*). `ease_of_use` = the reviewer found it easy or intuitive to operate (*직관적*, *편리*, *간편*). Heavily overlapping; reported separately **and** as a union (§3.1) so no finding depends on the split. | `6416619753` (*"딱 제가 원하는 기능만"*) = simplicity; `9819495909` (*"직관적인 인터페이스가 사용하기 편합니다"*) = ease |
| `question_to_developer` | Requires a question *directed at the developer*, not merely confusion. | `6964161904` (*"icloud 연동은 안되는건가요?"*) = assigned; `10371328962` (*"ui개 답답함"*) = `usability_negative` only |
| `low_info` | Assigned only when there is no extractable product signal anywhere in title **or** body. A title carrying signal disqualifies it. | `9409544493` (title *"굿"*, body *"굿"*) = `low_info`; `11923165739` (*"Great app to keep track of the days you do something"*) = `low_info` (describes the category, not the product) |
| A request inside praise | Assigned as a request regardless of star rating. 234 of the 267 request-carrying reviews (87.6%) are 4★ or 5★. | `8942549944` (5★) carries `req_icloud_sync` |
| `churn_uninstalled` | Requires an explicit statement of deleting or leaving, past or imminent. Threats conditional on a fix are not counted. | `10346163428` (*"몇년째쓰다가 삭제합니다"*) = assigned; `13014831178` (*"조치해주시면 별점 수정하겠습니다"*) = not assigned |
| `outcome_behavior_change` | Requires a stated change in behaviour or life, not an intention or a compliment. | `7062715389` (*"5개월 넘게 좋은습관들 이어 나가고 있습니다"*) = assigned; a bare *"최고"* would not be |
| `rating_text_contradiction` | Reserved for the four reviews where the rating and the body are *flatly* opposed. The 162 five-star reviews carrying a negative theme are **not** tagged, because at 36% of the segment that is the corpus norm, not an anomaly (§4.6). | `13299429338` (2★, *"I absolutely love this app"*) = assigned |

**Keyword audit of the hand map (§1.5 step 6).** Thirteen sweeps were run against the completed map. **19 genuine boundary errors were found and corrected**; all of them were *missing* themes, none was a wrongly-assigned one:

| Correction | Review | Theme added | Why the hand map missed it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `7028234235` (CA) | `ads_complaint` | *"I'd need only no-ads"* — an irritation stated as a purchase requirement |
| 2 | `3952229939` (KR) | `simplicity_minimal` | *"깔끔하고 너무 좋아요"* in the **title**; the body is a pure request |
| 3 | `4064393216` (KR) | `ease_of_use` | *"간편하고 좋아요"* |
| 4 | `4949407944` (KR) | `simplicity_minimal` | *"깔끔하고 좋은데"* preceding a feature request |
| 5 | `5370880957` (KR) | `ease_of_use` | *"앱 자체 기능이 좋고 편리하여"* |
| 6 | `5392075954` (KR) | `simplicity_minimal` | *"깔끔하고 좋은데"* in the title |
| 7 | `5515241435` (KR) | `intent_to_purchase` | *"프리미엄 기능으로라도 추가해주시면"* / "add it even as a Premium feature" — accepts paying |
| 8 | `5714521465` (KR) | `ease_of_use` | *"편리하고 좋은거같아서"* |
| 9 | `5950483738` (KR) | `feature_completeness_praise` | *"딱 원하는 기능이어서"* |
| 10–11 | `6087824080` (KR) | `ease_of_use`, `simplicity_minimal` | *"직관적이고 깔끔해서 너무 좋아요"*, buried inside an Android request |
| 12 | `6412826572` (KR) | `advocacy_recommend` | Title is *"강추합니다!"* / "strongly recommend!" |
| 13 | `6417185599` (KR) | `simplicity_minimal` | *"어플 깔끔하고 좋은데"* in the title |
| 14 | `6439970458` (KR) | `question_to_developer` | *"이게 프리미엄기능인건지 왜 안되는건가요"* |
| 15 | `6455120789` (KR) | `comparative_best` | *"다른 앱으로 갈아타기엔 이 어플이 너무 만족스럽고"* / "too satisfied here to switch" |
| 16 | `6604829212` (KR) | `comparative_best` | Title is *"정착했어요"* / "I've settled here" |
| 17 | `6692538174` (KR) | `simplicity_minimal` | *"넘 깔끔해서 맘에 드는데"* |
| 18 | `6943078588` (KR) | `simplicity_minimal` | *"깔끔하고 좋습니다"* |
| 19 | `7004263966` (KR) | `comparative_best` | *"아이폰갈아타면서 유목민생활하다가 체크를못해 찝찝하던 와중에.. 이앱을 발견했는데.. 오마이갓 너무 좋네요!"* — the nomad/settle pattern |
| 20 | `10678560230` (US) | `aesthetic_design` | *"this beautiful and useful app"* |

*(Nineteen corrections across twenty rows — `6087824080` received two themes.)*

**Every other audit hit was a regex false positive and is recorded here rather than silently dropped.** The recurring classes: `ads?` matching "iPad", "dreamed", "designed", "instead", "had", "load", "upgraded", "reached"; `원` matching 지**원** (support), 응**원** (cheer), 복**원** (restore); `watch` matching "watching an ad"; `패드` matching 키**패드** (keypad); `연동` matching "linking *with friends*" (`6866946633`) rather than device sync; `취소` matching "cancelled by an error" (`13878772549`); "way too much" matching a complaint about *features*, not price (`8241797483`).

**Why the map is hand-built.** Two illustrations from this corpus: (a) **six reviewers report the exact same defect with no shared vocabulary** — *"위젯에 아무것도 안뜨는데 (검정색)"*, *"위젯이 벽돌상태"*, *"화면이 갑자기 안 뜹니다"*, *"위젯 목록에 뜨질 않네요"*, *"Widgets Never Work"*, *"Cant not add widget"*; (b) **the praise sweep flagged 66 records containing praise vocabulary with no positive theme, and 51 of those were correct** — a bare *"좋아요"* attached to a bug report is not praise for any named attribute, and treating it as such would have inflated every UX theme by roughly 50%.

### Segment rates (labelled as such; never substituted for the global classification)

| Segment | Denominator | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| **Self-identified payers** | **99** | 31.31% report a purchase-side failure · 22.22% `post_purchase_failure` · 23.2% report a widget problem · 11.11% `ease_of_use` · 6.06% `price_fair_praise` · mean ★ 4.010 (below the corpus mean) |
| Payers, E1 (2019–21) | 31 | 4 report a purchase-side failure (12.9%) · mean ★ 4.258 |
| Payers, E2 (2022–23) | 22 | 6 (27.3%) · mean ★ 4.091 |
| Payers, E3 (2024–26) | 46 | **21 (45.7%)** · mean ★ 3.804 |
| Payers, 2026 only | 12 | **8 report a post-purchase failure** |
| Five-star reviews | 450 | **162 carry ≥1 negative-direction theme (36.00%)** · 35 ask the developer a question · 29 request sync · 26 report a regression |
| Four-star reviews | 156 | `paid_premium_confirmed` 17.9% — the payer's bucket |
| Three-star reviews | 72 | `price_too_high` 8.3% (3.2× the global rate) |
| Two-star reviews | 29 | `monetization_model_change_complaint` 10.3% (8× global) · `long_tenure_user` 10.3% (3.5× global) |
| One-star reviews | 72 | `churn_uninstalled` 27.8% · `ads_complaint` 25.0% · **`paid_premium_confirmed` 20.8%** · `post_purchase_failure` 18.1% |
| Stated uninstalls | 25 | **20 (80%) cite ads** · 0 cite a crash · 0 cite data loss · mean ★ 1.360 |
| Ads-any reviewers | 47 | 23 are 1★ (48.9%) · 20 uninstalled (42.6%) · mean ★ 2.234 |
| `ads_tolerable` reviewers | 6 | **all 6 gave 5★**; all describe a short ad confined to creation |
| Korea | 605 | `question_to_developer` 8.10% · `update_regression` 8.76% · `price_too_high` 0.99% · `ads_complaint` 2.48% · `churn_uninstalled` 2.15% |
| United States | 66 | `simplicity_minimal` 19.70% · `comparative_best` 18.18% · `ads_complaint` 15.15% · `churn_uninstalled` 15.15% |
| Japan ⚠️ limited evidence | 26 | `price_too_high` **26.92%** · `onboarding_no_guidance` 15.38% · `free_tier_cap` 11.54% · `comparative_best` 0.00% |
| High-spend group excluding KR | 128 | mean ★ 3.844 · `ads_complaint` 9.4% · `price_too_high` 8.6% · `churn_uninstalled` 7.8% |
| Non-KR | 174 | `price_too_high` 8.05% (8.1× KR) · `ads_complaint` 9.20% (3.7× KR) · `question_to_developer` 0.57% (0.07× KR) |

## 9.4 Counting rules, denominators and reconciliation summary

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Records in `reviews.jsonl` | **779** |
| Records read individually, in full, in original language | **779 (100%)** |
| Records classified | **779 (100%)** |
| Records with zero themes | **0** |
| Global denominator for every percentage | **779** |
| Themes | **163** |
| Theme assignments (total) | **1,899** |
| Themes per review | min 1 · max 10 · mean 2.44 |
| Themes with n ≥ 2 | 134 |
| Themes with n = 1 (all labelled "weak") | 29 |
| Unique review IDs | 779 — zero duplicates |
| Reconciliation to `by_country/*.jsonl` | 779 = 779, zero orphans either way, zero country mismatches |
| Reconciliation to `manifest.json` | Totals, per-storefront counts, rating distribution and mean all reproduce exactly (recomputed mean 4.13350) |
| Storefronts queried (`_state.json`) | 69, all `complete: true` |
| Storefronts with ≥1 review | 29 |
| Storefronts returning zero reviews | 40 |
| Deduplication performed | **None** — zero exact title+body duplicate pairs exist |
| Review IDs cited anywhere in this report | **Verified to exist in `reviews.jsonl` — 100%** (§9.7) |

**Signal threshold table used throughout:** `<0.1%` ignore · `0.1–0.5%` weak · `0.5–1%` emerging · `1–3%` meaningful · `3–5%` very strong · `>5%` high-priority. **At n = 779 the "ignore" band is unreachable** (one review = 0.128%), which is stated at §3 and §9.1.

**Country denominators used for country-level signal labels:** Korea 605, United States 66. **No other storefront is used as a denominator for a standalone claim.** Japan (26), Canada (12), Great Britain (10) and all smaller storefronts appear only with an explicit limited-evidence label.

**Severe-harm exception applied once:** `bug_data_loss` (12 reviews, 1.54%, "meaningful") is promoted in §3.3.4, §8.1 and §8.2 above its share, under the policy's data-loss exception, because it concerns irrecoverable loss of user history and because recovery is Premium-gated (`11222810326`, `11103760055`).

**Themes deliberately *not* promoted despite low counts:** the 29 singleton themes at §9.2 rows 135–163. Each is recorded with its ID and labelled weak. None appears in Part 8 as a recommendation on its own; three are cited as supporting colour alongside larger themes (`accessibility_concern` with `req_dark_mode`; `misleading_premium_claims` with `post_purchase_failure`; `subscription_request` with `price_unbundling_request`).

## 9.5 External sources used

| Source | Accessed | Confidence | Used for | Used against |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Publicly reported App Store release notes for app ID `1456241316` — **store page egress-blocked; search-engine summary only** | 12 Sep 2026 | **Low–medium** | §2.3 and §5.5 corroboration of the v3.1.0 Premium-activation defect and the v3.1.1 fix for applying Premium and restoring purchases; confirmation that the listing advertises widgets as a headline feature and mentions subscriptions + Family Sharing | Nothing in Parts 3, 4, 6, 7 |
| Publicly reported App Store consumer-spend market rankings | 12 Sep 2026 | **Low–medium** | Defining high-spend group membership in §6.6 only (US, JP, GB, DE, FR, CA, AU, KR, CN) | Any revenue claim about this app |

**Three verification attempts failed and are disclosed rather than worked around:** `itunes.apple.com/lookup`, `apps.apple.com` (both US and KR storefronts) and a candidate developer domain all return an egress-proxy block (HTTP 403 at the CONNECT tunnel) from this environment. **No primary store metadata — current price, IAP list, version history, release date, rating count — could be read.** Every price, gate, cap and capability statement in Part 2 is sourced to a named review ID and a date.

**No finding in Parts 3–8 rests on an external source.** Where external material corroborates an internal finding (§5.5), that is stated as corroboration and the internal evidence is given first and in full.

## 9.7 Final verification — run against the finished report

Every check below was executed against the completed Markdown file, not against an intermediate draft (`temp/57-verify-report.py`, `temp/57-verify-quotes.py`, `temp/57-verify-quotes2.py`).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Review IDs mentioned anywhere in this report | **3,240 mentions, 779 unique** |
| **Cited IDs that do not exist in `reviews.jsonl`** | **0 ✓** (the only other 10-digit backticked number in the file is the App Store ID `1456241316`) |
| **Corpus records never cited anywhere in this report** | **0 ✓** — all 779 appear |
| §9.6 index rows | **779 rows, 779 unique, set-identical to `reviews.jsonl` ✓** |
| §9.6 rows whose storefront / rating / date disagree with the source record | **0 ✓** |
| Inline `` `ID`(store,N★) `` attributions cross-checked against the source record | **1,901 checked · 0 errors ✓** |
| Inline `` `ID` (STORE, N★) `` prose attributions cross-checked | **6 checked · 0 errors ✓** |
| Inline `` `ID` (STORE) `` prose attributions cross-checked | **32 checked · 0 errors ✓** |
| Quoted review excerpts verified verbatim against the corpus (whitespace-insensitive; each side of an ellipsis checked separately) | **302 fragments · 299 verbatim review quotes ✓, 3 non-review (next row)** |
| Non-review quotations excluded from that check and identified here | 3 — the external release-notes summary (§2.3, §5.5) and two occurrences of the analyst's own phrase *"One person said this."* (§3.4, §9.7) |
| Classification map re-validated after the final edit | **✓ 779/779 classified, 0 unknown IDs, 0 intra-theme duplicates, 163 themes, 1,899 assignments** |

**Inexact quotes found and corrected during verification — 17 in total.** Each was re-read against the source record and restored:

| Review | What was wrong | Restored to |
|---|---|---|
| `6522788831` | dropped the source's own typo | *"wins **in** for the insightful data"* |
| `9349167270` | dropped the source's own typo | *"I'd like **his but** fixed or a refund"* (marked [sic]) |
| `5627754008` | Traditional 買 for the source's Simplified 买 | *"**买**断费用稍贵"* |
| `11241868525` | unmarked elision | *"…나네요.. **적당히 해야지** 한달에 5~6번은…"* |
| `13958683782` | dropped a word | *"습관 **중** 하나 횟수 조정하고"* |
| `6262697602` | unmarked elision of a sentence | ellipsis inserted |
| `8772322659` | added a space | *"**다른거** 써봐도"* |
| `5947186533` | dropped a suffix | *"개발자분**들** 복 받으세요"* |
| `12304194492` | merged title and body loosely | body quoted in full |
| `13014831178` | merged title and body, unmarked elision | title and body given separately, elision marked |
| `10678560230` | dropped three words | *"possible **to be able** to have unlimited widget options"* |
| `6181430289` | rewrote the reviewer's spacing and dropped a parenthetical | restored in full |
| `5611409056` | wrong opening words | *"**잘** 만들어주셔서 프리미엄도 구매했네요**ㅎㅎ**"* |
| `9800909083` | merged title and body | title and body given separately |
| `9562235582` | added a full stop the source lacks | trailing period removed |
| `7004263966` | unmarked elision | restored in full |
| `11241868525` (§9.1 vocabulary list) | clipped mid-word | *"위젯**이** 벽돌상태"* |

**Quotations that span a review's title and body** are marked with ` ⁄ ` between the two parts (`13014831178`, `9800909083`). Three others are quoted as a single run because the title is a grammatical sentence that continues into the body and the join is unambiguous: `14352377997` (*"I paid for the premium and now it asks for subscription??!"* / *"DO NOT BUY IT"*), `11929068665`, `13592161917`. **No word was added, removed or reordered in any of them.**

**Translations** appear for every non-English quotation. They are the analyst's, are marked with `/`, and the original is always given first. Korean internet abbreviations and emoticons (ㅠㅠ, ㅎㅎ, ㅜㅜ, 넘, 짱, 유목민) are rendered for sense, not literally, and the original text is always present for audit.

---

**End of report.** Corpus: 779 reviews · 29 storefronts · 30 March 2019 – 27 August 2026 · all 779 read individually in their original language · 163 hand-assigned themes · 1,899 theme assignments · every claim traceable to §9.3 and §9.6.
